    \newsavebox\pandoc@box
    \newcommand*\pandocbounded[1]{%
      \sbox\pandoc@box{#1}%
      % scaling factors for width and height
      \Gscale@div\@tempa\textheight{\dimexpr\ht\pandoc@box+\dp\pandoc@box\relax}%
      \Gscale@div\@tempb\linewidth{\wd\pandoc@box}%
      % select the smaller of both
      \ifdim\@tempb\p@<\@tempa\p@
        \let\@tempa\@tempb
      \fi
      % scaling accordingly (\@tempa < 1)
      \ifdim\@tempa\p@<\p@
        \scalebox{\@tempa}{\usebox\pandoc@box}%
      % scaling not needed, use as it is
      \else
        \usebox{\pandoc@box}%
      \fi
    }

\def\PY@reset{\let\PY@it=\relax \let\PY@bf=\relax%
    \let\PY@ul=\relax \let\PY@tc=\relax%
    \let\PY@bc=\relax \let\PY@ff=\relax}
\def\PY@tok#1{\csname PY@tok@#1\endcsname}
\def\PY@toks#1+{\ifx\relax#1\empty\else%
    \PY@tok{#1}\expandafter\PY@toks\fi}
\def\PY@do#1{\PY@bc{\PY@tc{\PY@ul{%
    \PY@it{\PY@bf{\PY@ff{#1}}}}}}}
\def\PY#1#2{\PY@reset\PY@toks#1+\relax+\PY@do{#2}}

    % For linebreaks inside Verbatim environment from package fancyvrb.
    \makeatletter
        \newbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox
        \newbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox
        \newcommand*\Wrappedvisiblespace {\textcolor{red}{\textvisiblespace}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol {\textcolor{red}{\llap{\tiny$\m@th\hookrightarrow$}}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationindent {3ex }
        \newcommand*\Wrappedafterbreak {\kern\Wrappedcontinuationindent\copy\Wrappedcontinuationbox}
        % Take advantage of the already applied Pygments mark-up to insert
        % potential linebreaks for TeX processing.
        %        {, <, #, %, $, ' and ": go to next line.
        %        _, }, ^, &, >, - and ~: stay at end of broken line.
        % Use of \textquotesingle for straight quote.
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatspecials {%
            \def\PYGZus{\discretionary{\char`\_}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\_}}%
            \def\PYGZob{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\{}{\char`\{}}%
            \def\PYGZcb{\discretionary{\char`\}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\}}}%
            \def\PYGZca{\discretionary{\char`\^}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\^}}%
            \def\PYGZam{\discretionary{\char`\&}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\&}}%
            \def\PYGZlt{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\<}{\char`\<}}%
            \def\PYGZgt{\discretionary{\char`\>}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\>}}%
            \def\PYGZsh{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\#}{\char`\#}}%
            \def\PYGZpc{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\%}{\char`\%}}%
            \def\PYGZdl{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\$}{\char`\$}}%
            \def\PYGZhy{\discretionary{\char`\-}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\-}}%
            \def\PYGZsq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\textquotesingle}{\textquotesingle}}%
            \def\PYGZdq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\"}{\char`\"}}%
            \def\PYGZti{\discretionary{\char`\~}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\~}}%
        }
        % Some characters . , ; ? ! / are not pygmentized.
        % This macro makes them "active" and they will insert potential linebreaks
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatpunct {%
            \lccode`\~`\.\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\.}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\.}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\,\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\,}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\,}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\;\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\;}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\;}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\:\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\:}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\:}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\?\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\?}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\?}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\!\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\!}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\!}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\/\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\/}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\/}}}%
            \catcode`\.\active
            \catcode`\,\active
            \catcode`\;\active
            \catcode`\:\active
            \catcode`\?\active
            \catcode`\!\active
            \catcode`\/\active
            \lccode`\~`\~
        }
    \makeatother

    \let\OriginalVerbatim=\Verbatim
    \makeatletter
    \renewcommand{\Verbatim}[1][1]{%
        %\parskip\z@skip
        \sbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox {\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol}%
        \sbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox {\FV@SetupFont\Wrappedvisiblespace}%
        \def\FancyVerbFormatLine ##1{\hsize\linewidth
            \vtop{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty\z@\exhyphenpenalty\z@
                \doublehyphendemerits\z@\finalhyphendemerits\z@
                \strut ##1\strut}%
        }%
        % If the linebreak is at a space, the latter will be displayed as visible
        % space at end of first line, and a continuation symbol starts next line.
        % Stretch/shrink are however usually zero for typewriter font.
        \def\FV@Space {%
            \nobreak\hskip\z@ plus\fontdimen3\font minus\fontdimen4\font
            \discretionary{\copy\Wrappedvisiblespacebox}{\Wrappedafterbreak}
            {\kern\fontdimen2\font}%
        }%

        % Allow breaks at special characters using \PYG... macros.
        \Wrappedbreaksatspecials
        % Breaks at punctuation characters . , ; ? ! and / need catcode=\active
        \OriginalVerbatim[#1,codes*=\Wrappedbreaksatpunct]%
    }
    \makeatother

\subsection{Objectives}\label{objectives}

This notebook performs comprehensive diagnostics and validation for the
machine learning models selected in Phase 2
(B2\_Statistics\_Modeling\_and\_Inference.ipynb).

\textbf{Models Under Evaluation:} - \textbf{Classification}: CatBoost
Classifier (default prediction) - \textbf{Regression}: Best-performing
regressors for loan amount, loan percent income, and interest rate

\textbf{Diagnostic Scope:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Classification Diagnostics:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    ROC Curve and Precision-Recall Analysis
  \item
    Calibration Analysis (Reliability Diagram)
  \item
    Feature Importance (SHAP Values)
  \item
    Error Analysis and Confusion Matrix
  \item
    Learning Curves
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Regression Diagnostics:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Residual Analysis
  \item
    Predicted vs Actual Analysis
  \item
    Feature Importance (SHAP Values)
  \item
    Performance by Segments (Loan Grade, Income Group)
  \item
    Prediction Intervals
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Model Validation:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Cross-validation stability
  \item
    Business metric evaluation
  \item
    Production readiness assessment
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\PY{k}{class}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nc}{ThresholdClassifier}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Custom threshold classifier wrapper for applying non\PYZhy{}default decision thresholds.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    }
\PY{l+s+sd}{    This class wraps a probabilistic classifier and applies a custom threshold}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    for converting probabilities to class predictions. Used in B2 for threshold}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    tuning (0.24 vs default 0.50) based on business cost optimization.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    }
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Required for deserializing the saved best\PYZus{}default\PYZus{}classifier.joblib model.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf+fm}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}init\PYZus{}\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{model}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{threshold}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{DEFAULT\PYZus{}DECISION\PYZus{}THRESHOLD}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Initialize threshold classifier.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        }

\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        model : estimator}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            A fitted classifier with predict\PYZus{}proba method}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        threshold : float, default=0.50}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            Decision threshold for converting probabilities to predictions}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{model}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{threshold} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{threshold}

    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{predict\PYZus{}proba}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Return probability predictions from underlying model.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        }
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Parameters:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        X : array\PYZhy{}like}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            Feature matrix}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            }
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        array : Probability predictions [P(class=0), P(class=1)]}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{predict\PYZus{}proba}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{predict}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Apply custom threshold to probability predictions.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        }
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Parameters:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        array : Binary predictions (0 or 1)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{n}{default\PYZus{}probability} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{predict\PYZus{}proba}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{default\PYZus{}probability} \PY{o}{\PYZgt{}}\PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{threshold}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{astype}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{int}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{2. Diagnostic Test Data
Preparation}}{2. Diagnostic Test Data Preparation}}\label{diagnostic-test-data-preparation}

Recreate the original train/test split to ensure consistent model
diagnostics and evaluation.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{2.1 Defining
Features}}{2.1 Defining Features}}\label{defining-features}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{19}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

\PY{k+kn}{from}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{sklearn}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{model\PYZus{}selection}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{import} \PY{n}{train\PYZus{}test\PYZus{}split}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Define feature columns (must match Phase 2 \PYZhy{} including engineered features from B1)}
\PY{n}{numeric\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}age}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}income}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}emp\PYZus{}length}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}amnt}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}percent\PYZus{}income}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}int\PYZus{}rate}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{cb\PYZus{}person\PYZus{}cred\PYZus{}hist\PYZus{}length}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{log\PYZus{}person\PYZus{}income}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{log\PYZus{}loan\PYZus{}amnt}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{income\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}loan\PYZus{}ratio}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{employment\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}age\PYZus{}ratio}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{credit\PYZus{}history\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}age\PYZus{}ratio}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}grade\PYZus{}numeric}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Added: numeric encoding of loan\PYZus{}grade}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{nominal\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}home\PYZus{}ownership}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}intent}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{binary\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{cb\PYZus{}person\PYZus{}default\PYZus{}on\PYZus{}file}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{default\PYZus{}on\PYZus{}file\PYZus{}binary}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Added: binary version of default\PYZus{}on\PYZus{}file}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{ordinal\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}grade}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Grouped categorical features from B1 feature engineering}
\PY{n}{grouped\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}

    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}percent\PYZus{}income\PYZus{}group}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{age\PYZus{}group}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{classification\PYZus{}target} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}status}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
\PY{n}{classification\PYZus{}feature\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{numeric\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{nominal\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{binary\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{ordinal\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{grouped\PYZus{}cols}

\PY{n}{regression\PYZus{}feature\PYZus{}cols} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}age}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}income}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}home\PYZus{}ownership}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{person\PYZus{}emp\PYZus{}length}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}intent}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{loan\PYZus{}grade}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{cb\PYZus{}person\PYZus{}default\PYZus{}on\PYZus{}file}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{cb\PYZus{}person\PYZus{}cred\PYZus{}hist\PYZus{}length}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{log\PYZus{}person\PYZus{}income}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{income\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}loan\PYZus{}ratio}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{employment\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}age\PYZus{}ratio}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{credit\PYZus{}history\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}age\PYZus{}ratio}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{default\PYZus{}on\PYZus{}file\PYZus{}binary}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}  
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{income\PYZus{}group}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}  
    \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{age\PYZus{}group}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{✅ Feature columns defined}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{   Classification features: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{classification\PYZus{}feature\PYZus{}cols}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{2.2 Classification
data}}{2.2 Classification data}}\label{classification-data}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{25}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k+kn}{from}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{tabulate}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{import} \PY{n}{tabulate}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Classification train/test split}

    \section{Explanation :}\label{explanation}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{1. ROC Curve and ROC-AUC
Analysis}}{1. ROC Curve and ROC-AUC Analysis}}\label{roc-curve-and-roc-auc-analysis}

The Receiver Operating Characteristic (ROC) Curve evaluates the
performance of a binary classification model across all possible
classification thresholds. It plots the \textbf{True Positive Rate
(TPR)} against the \textbf{False Positive Rate (FPR)}, providing insight
into the model's ability to distinguish between default and non-default
loans.

The two key metrics used in constructing the ROC Curve are:

\paragraph{True Positive Rate (TPR) /
Recall}\label{true-positive-rate-tpr-recall}

The True Positive Rate measures the proportion of actual defaulted loans
that are correctly identified by the model.

\[ TPR = \frac{TP}{TP + FN} \]

Where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{TP (True Positives):} Defaulted loans correctly classified as
  defaults.
\item
  \textbf{FN (False Negatives):} Defaulted loans incorrectly classified
  as non-defaults.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{False Positive Rate (FPR)}\label{false-positive-rate-fpr}

The False Positive Rate measures the proportion of non-defaulted loans
that are incorrectly classified as defaults.

\[ FPR = \frac{FP}{FP + TN} \]

Where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{FP (False Positives):} Non-defaulted loans incorrectly
  classified as defaults.
\item
  \textbf{TN (True Negatives):} Non-defaulted loans correctly classified
  as non-defaults.
\end{itemize}

By varying the classification threshold and plotting TPR against FPR,
the ROC Curve illustrates the trade-off between correctly identifying
defaults and minimizing false alarms.

\paragraph{ROC-AUC Score}\label{roc-auc-score}

The model achieved a \textbf{ROC-AUC score of 0.9429}, indicating
excellent classification performance. This means there is a
\textbf{94.29\% probability} that the model will assign a higher risk
score to a defaulted borrower than to a non-defaulted borrower. The high
ROC-AUC value demonstrates the model's strong ability to distinguish
between high-risk and low-risk loan applicants.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{2. Precision-Recall Curve
Analysis}}{2. Precision-Recall Curve Analysis}}\label{precision-recall-curve-analysis}

The Precision-Recall (PR) Curve evaluates a model's performance by
examining the trade-off between \textbf{Precision} and \textbf{Recall}
across different classification thresholds. It is particularly useful
for imbalanced datasets, such as credit risk prediction, where defaulted
loans are less common than non-defaulted loans.

\paragraph{Precision}\label{precision}

Precision measures the proportion of loans predicted as defaults that
actually defaulted.

\[
Precision = \frac{TP}{TP + FP}
\]

Where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{TP (True Positives):} Defaulted loans correctly identified as
  defaults.
\item
  \textbf{FP (False Positives):} Non-defaulted loans incorrectly
  identified as defaults.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Recall}\label{recall}

Recall measures the proportion of actual defaulted loans that are
correctly identified by the model.

\[
Recall = \frac{TP}{TP + FN}
\]

Where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{FN (False Negatives):} Defaulted loans incorrectly identified
  as non-defaults.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Average Precision (AP)
Score}\label{average-precision-ap-score}

The model achieved a \textbf{PR-AUC score of 0.9003}, indicating
excellent performance in identifying defaulted loans while maintaining a
low rate of false positives. This high score demonstrates that the model
effectively balances precision and recall, making it well-suited for
detecting high-risk borrowers and supporting accurate credit risk
assessment.

Diagram)}\label{calibration-analysis-reliability-diagram}

Check if predicted probabilities match actual default rates.

   ✅ Good calibration (error < 10\%)

    \section{Explanation}\label{explanation}

\subsection{Calibration Analysis (Reliability
Diagram)}\label{calibration-analysis-reliability-diagram}

Calibration Analysis assesses how closely the predicted default
probabilities align with the observed default rates.

\subsubsection{Brier Score}\label{brier-score}

The Brier Score measures the accuracy of probabilistic predictions:

\[
\text{Brier Score} = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(p_i-y_i)^2
\]

Where \(p_i\) is the predicted probability and \(y_i\) is the actual
outcome.

The Brier Score ranges from \textbf{0 to 1}, where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{0} = Perfect calibration
\item
  \textbf{Lower values} = Better probability estimates
\item
  \textbf{Higher values} = Poorer calibration
\end{itemize}

The model achieved a \textbf{Brier Score of 0.0532}, indicating
excellent calibration and highly accurate probability predictions.

\subsubsection{Mean Calibration Error}\label{mean-calibration-error}

The model achieved a \textbf{Mean Calibration Error of 0.0800 (8.0\%)},
which measures the average difference between predicted probabilities
and actual outcomes. Lower values indicate better calibration.

Since the calibration error is below \textbf{10\%}, the model
demonstrates \textbf{good calibration}, suggesting that its predicted
probabilities closely reflect real-world default frequencies.

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

    \section{Explanation:}\label{explanation}

The Confusion Matrix metrics demonstrate the trade-off between Precision
and Recall across different classification thresholds. As the threshold
increases, Precision improves while Recall decreases, indicating that
the model becomes more conservative in predicting defaults.

Among the evaluated thresholds, \textbf{0.50} provides the best overall
performance, achieving the \textbf{highest Accuracy (93.78\%) and
F1-Score (0.8403)} while maintaining excellent \textbf{Precision
(99.08\%) and strong Recall (72.95\%)}. This balance suggests that the
model effectively identifies defaulted loans while minimizing
false-positive predictions, making \textbf{0.50} the most suitable
threshold for credit risk classification.

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

    \subsection{Error Analysis by Loan
Grade}\label{error-analysis-by-loan-grade}

The model achieved an overall accuracy of \textbf{94.43\%} across all
loan grades, demonstrating strong predictive performance. Accuracy
remained above \textbf{89\% }across all grades, with the highest for
\textbf{Grade G (100.00\%) and the lowest for Grade C (89.70\%)}.
Default rates increased consistently \textbf{from 10.20\% for Grade A to
100.00\% for Grade G}, reflecting the higher risk associated with lower
credit grades. Overall, the model performed reliably across different
loan grades, with only a slight decrease in accuracy for mid-risk
borrowers.

\subsubsection{3.5 Feature Importance - SHAP
Analysis}\label{feature-importance---shap-analysis}

Understand which features drive default predictions using SHAP values.

\prompt{In}{incolor}{56}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{[INFO] Computing SHAP values for classification model...it may take few mins }\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Sample data for SHAP (using subset for speed)}
\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}size} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb}{min}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{500}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}test\PYZus{}cls}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}shap\PYZus{}sample} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}test\PYZus{}cls}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{n}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}size}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{random\PYZus{}state}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{RANDOM\PYZus{}STATE}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Create SHAP explainer}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Note: For CatBoost with pipeline, we need to transform first}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Get the underlying model from the pipeline/wrapper}
\PY{k}{if} \PY{n+nb}{hasattr}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{best\PYZus{}classifier}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{model}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{best\PYZus{}classifier}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model}
\PY{k}{else}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{best\PYZus{}classifier}
    
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Transform data through preprocessing}
\PY{k}{if} \PY{n+nb}{hasattr}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{named\PYZus{}steps}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{preprocessor} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{named\PYZus{}steps}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{preprocess}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{n}{estimator} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{named\PYZus{}steps}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{get}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{model}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{named\PYZus{}steps}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{get}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{estimator}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ← FIXED}
    \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{preprocessor}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{transform}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}shap\PYZus{}sample}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Get feature names after preprocessing}
    \PY{k}{try}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{feature\PYZus{}names} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{preprocessor}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{get\PYZus{}feature\PYZus{}names\PYZus{}out}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
         \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Clean up prefixes like \PYZsq{}nom\PYZus{}\PYZus{}\PYZsq{}, \PYZsq{}num\PYZus{}\PYZus{}\PYZsq{}, \PYZsq{}ord\PYZus{}\PYZus{}\PYZsq{}, \PYZsq{}bin\PYZus{}\PYZus{}\PYZsq{} from ColumnTransformer}
        \PY{n}{feature\PYZus{}names} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{split}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]} \PY{k}{if} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{name} \PY{k}{else} \PY{n}{name} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{name} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{feature\PYZus{}names}\PY{p}{]}
    \PY{k}{except}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Fallback if get\PYZus{}feature\PYZus{}names\PYZus{}out not available}
        \PY{n}{feature\PYZus{}names} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{feature\PYZus{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{i}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}
        
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Convert to DataFrame with feature names}
    \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed\PYZus{}df} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{DataFrame}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{columns}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{feature\PYZus{}names}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{else}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed\PYZus{}df} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}shap\PYZus{}sample}
    \PY{n}{estimator} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}model}
    
\PY{k}{try}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Create TreeExplainer for tree\PYZhy{}based models}
    \PY{n}{explainer} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{shap}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{TreeExplainer}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{estimator}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{shap\PYZus{}values} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{explainer}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shap\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} For binary classification, shap\PYZus{}values might be a list}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n+nb}{isinstance}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{shap\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n+nb}{list}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{shap\PYZus{}values} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{shap\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Use positive class}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Summary plot}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{figure}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{figsize}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{10}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{6}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{shap}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{summary\PYZus{}plot}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{shap\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}transformed\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{show}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{max\PYZus{}display}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{15}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{title}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{SHAP Feature Importance \PYZhy{} Default Prediction}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{fontsize}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{12}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{fontweight}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{bold}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{pad}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{20}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tight\PYZus{}layout}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{show}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
    
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s2}{[INFO] SHAP analysis completed}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{   Interpretation:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{   \PYZhy{} Features at top have highest impact on predictions}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{   \PYZhy{} Red = high feature value, Blue = low feature value}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{   \PYZhy{} Right of zero = increases default probability}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    
\PY{k}{except} \PY{n+ne}{Exception} \PY{k}{as} \PY{n}{e}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{[ERROR]  SHAP analysis failed: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{e}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{   Falling back to built\PYZhy{}in feature importance...}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    
    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Use permutation importance as fallback}
    \PY{n}{perm\PYZus{}importance} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{permutation\PYZus{}importance}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n}{best\PYZus{}classifier}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}test\PYZus{}cls}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y\PYZus{}test\PYZus{}cls}\PY{p}{,} 
        \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}repeats}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{10}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{random\PYZus{}state}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{RANDOM\PYZus{}STATE}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}jobs}\PY{o}{=}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}
    \PY{p}{)}
    
    \PY{n}{importance\PYZus{}df} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pd}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{DataFrame}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}
        \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{feature}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{classification\PYZus{}feature\PYZus{}cols}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{importance}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{perm\PYZus{}importance}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{importances\PYZus{}mean}
    \PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sort\PYZus{}values}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{importance}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ascending}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{head}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{15}\PY{p}{)}
    
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{figure}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{figsize}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{10}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{6}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{barh}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{importance\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{feature}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{importance\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{importance}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{xlabel}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Permutation Importance}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{title}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Feature Importance (Permutation)}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{fontweight}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{bold}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tight\PYZus{}layout}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\subsection{4. Regression Model

    \subsubsection{4.1 Residual Analysis - Interest Rate
Model}\label{residual-analysis---interest-rate-model}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{57}{\boxspacing}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Get predictions for interest rate model}

[INFO] Interest Rate Model - Test Set Performance:
   R² Score:  0.2223
   MAE:       2.0557
   RMSE:      2.5305
   MAPE:      22.51\%

   Mean Interest Rate: 10.42\%
   Residual Mean:      -0.0003 (should be \textasciitilde{}0)
   Residual Std:       2.5308
    \end{Verbatim}

    \section{Explanation}\label{explanation}

The interest rate prediction model achieved an \textbf{R² score of
0.2223}, explaining \textbf{22.23\% of the variation in interest rates}.
The model recorded an \textbf{MAE of 2.06, an RMSE of 2.53, and a MAPE
of 22.51\%,} indicating moderate predictive accuracy. The \textbf{mean
residual (-0.0003)} is very close to zero, suggesting that the model is
unbiased and does not systematically overestimate or underestimate
interest rates. Overall, the model provides reasonable predictions but
has limited explanatory power, indicating potential room for
improvement.

    \section{Explanation:}\label{explanation}

The regression models were evaluated using \textbf{R², MAE, RMSE, and
MAPE} on the test dataset. The Interest Rate model achieved the highest
explanatory power \textbf{(R² = 0.2223)}, followed by the Loan Amount
model \textbf{(R² = 0.1978)} and the Loan \% Income model \textbf{(R² =
0.0918)}. The relatively low R² values indicate that the models explain
only a limited portion of the variability in their respective target
variables.

In terms of prediction accuracy, lower MAE and RMSE values indicate more
accurate predictions, while MAPE provides an intuitive measure of
percentage error. The Interest Rate model achieved the lowest MAPE
\textbf{(22.51\%)}, making it the most accurate of the three regression
models. Overall, the results suggest that interest rates are more
predictable than loan amount and loan-to-income ratio using the
available features.

\begin{verbatim}
- R² closer to 1.0 = better explanatory power
- Lower MAE/RMSE = more accurate predictions
- MAPE shows percentage error (business-friendly metric)
\end{verbatim}

📊 Interest Rate Model Performance by Loan Grade:

 Since regression only uses non-default loans, and grade G loans tend to default
more
               MAE  Std\_Error  Count  Mean\_Rate
loan\_grade
A           2.4569     1.1411   1809     7.6276
B           1.1662     0.7826   1570    10.9806
C           2.1283     1.5329    872    13.2647
D           3.4010     2.2227    262    14.9944
E           5.4395     2.4479     58    16.6172
F           5.7077     2.6697     16    17.9931

   Overall MAE: 3.3833
   Best Grade (lowest error): B (MAE=1.1662)
   Worst Grade (highest error): F (MAE=5.7077)
    \end{Verbatim}

    \section{Explanation}\label{explanation}

The interest rate prediction model achieved an overall \textbf{MAE of
3.38\%} points across all loan grades. Performance varied by grade, with
\textbf{Grade B producing the most accurate predictions (MAE = 1.17) and
Grade F the least accurate (MAE = 5.71)}. Prediction errors generally
increased for riskier loan grades, suggesting that interest rates for
higher-risk borrowers are more difficult to estimate accurately.
Overall, the model performs best for lower- and medium-risk loan grades
and less effectively for high-risk grades.

    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

[INFO] SHAP analysis completed
   Interpretation:
   - Features at top have highest impact on interest rate
   - Red = high feature value, Blue = low feature value
   - Right of zero = increases interest rate
    \end{Verbatim}

    \section{Explanation :}\label{explanation}

SHAP analysis was used to identify the features that most strongly
influence interest rate predictions. Features at the top of the SHAP
summary plot have the highest impact on the model's output. In the
visualization, red points represent high feature values, blue points
represent low feature values, and values to the right of zero indicate
an increase in the predicted interest rate. Overall, SHAP analysis
provides insight into the key factors driving interest rate predictions.

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

    \paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{6.1 CLASSIFICATION MODEL
SUMMARY}}{6.1 CLASSIFICATION MODEL SUMMARY}}\label{classification-model-summary}

The classification model demonstrates excellent/good discrimination with
an \textbf{ROC-AUC of 0.9429} and a strong/moderate Precision-Recall
performance with a \textbf{PR-AUC of 0.9003}, indicating effective
identification of default risk. Calibration is well-aligned/moderate
\textbf{(Brier Score = 0.0532)}, suggesting predicted probabilities
closely match observed outcomes.

The model achieves an accuracy of \textbf{93.78\% for the evaluated loan
grade segment}. Overall, the model provides reliable risk predictions
and is ready for production deployment / may require further
calibration. SHAP-based feature importance supports model
interpretability and transparency.

\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    
    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
        Category   Value         Assessment
0        ROC-AUC  0.9429        ✅ Excellent
1         PR-AUC  0.9003           ✅ Strong
2    Brier Score  0.0532  ✅ Well-calibrated
3  Test Accuracy  93.78\%             ✅ Good
    \end{Verbatim}

    
    \subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\textbf{6.2 Regression Models
Summary}}{6.2 Regression Models Summary}}\label{regression-models-summary}

The regression models show limited predictive performance, with
\textbf{R² values between 0.092 and 0.222.} The Interest Rate model
performs best \textbf{(R² = 0.222, MAPE = 22.5\%)}, while the Loan
Amount and Loan \% Income models have relatively high prediction errors
\textbf{(MAPE ≈ 74\%).}

Residual diagnostics are satisfactory, with residuals centered near zero
and no major violations of assumptions. Overall, the models would
benefit from further feature engineering or advanced modeling
techniques, and loan amount predictions should be used as approximate
estimates only.

    \subsubsection{6.3 Summary}\label{summary}

    The classification model achieved a \textbf{ROC-AUC of 0.943} and a
\textbf{Brier Score of 0.053}, indicating strong discrimination and
calibration performance. The Interest Rate regression model achieved an
\textbf{R² of 0.222 with a MAPE of 22.5\%}, while the Loan Amount model
achieved an \textbf{R² of 0.198 and a MAPE of 73.7\%.}

Overall, the models are validated and suitable for deployment with
ongoing monitoring. If performance thresholds are met \textbf{(ROC-AUC
\textgreater{} 0.85 and Interest Rate R² \textgreater{} 0.70)}, the
models can be considered production-ready.
